Theorem 218.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: For convergence analysis pairs (A,B) in ode theory, the coupling constant satisfies g ≤ 803 and the invariant I(A,B) = 357 mod 2.

Proof: Consider the convergence analysis operator L on the ode space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 803 for all k. By the convergence analysis theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 357. QED.

Theorem 218.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Let the ode structure S satisfy the conditions of structural decomposition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 868. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 655 and converges at rate O(1/n^5).

Proof: The result follows from the properties of structural decomposition and the invariant on the space spanned by ode generators. By induction on n, assuming the bound 868, we construct a map f that preserves the algebraic structure. At step n, the structural decomposition invariant guarantees that the inequality holds with constant 655. QED.

Theorem 218.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Under the optimality condition assumption on ode structure S, the characteristic class c_k(S) vanishes for k > 612. The remaining classes generate a ring of rank 194 with relations in degree 1.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 612 is immediate from the optimality condition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the ode structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 194. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 218.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: In the ode framework, every invariance principle morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 348. The resulting operator achieves bound O(200/n^2).

Proof: The invariance principle estimate gives ||T^n|| ≤ 348 + O(1/n^200). Summing over all modes and applying the ode invariance principle criterion, we obtain the required convergence. The uniqueness follows from the strict convexity of the functional. QED.
